O desktop pet está funcionando: a Kurumi anda sobre o topo das janelas e da barra de tarefas, escala as laterais para chegar mais alto e usa apenas as vistas de frente, lado e costas. Testei tanto na sua tela real quanto numa simulação acelerada de 60 minutos, que não encontrou nenhum erro de posicionamento.

![Escalando a lateral do Notepad](J:\meus app\Desktop Pets\preview\c_s_17.png)

**Como rodar:** na pasta do projeto, execute `npm start`. Para fechar, clique com o botão direito no ícone dela na bandeja do sistema e escolha "Sair". Esse é o único item de menu: sem ele não haveria como fechar o app.

**O que ela faz:**
- **Chão:** anda sobre a parte visível do topo de cada janela. Se uma janela na frente cobre parte do topo de outra, ela não pisa ali. A barra de tarefas funciona como chão base.
- **Escalar:** quando passa pela lateral de uma janela, pode subir por ela, vista de costas, e depois sobe no topo. Se a lateral começa um pouco acima de onde ela está (por exemplo, uma janela flutuando acima da barra de tarefas), ela pula para se agarrar.
- **Na borda de uma janela:** ela desce pela lateral, pula para baixo ou dá meia-volta. Se a janela sob ela for movida, ela acompanha; se for minimizada ou fechada, ela cai.
- **Outras ações:** fica parada, corre, senta (de lado), dorme deitada e faz gestos como acenar e apontar.
- **Tela cheia:** ela se esconde quando um jogo ou vídeo ocupa a tela inteira.

**Sobre o modelo:** uso os arquivos já extraídos em `DaL_Kurumi` (geometria, textura e animações), não o `.ysm` diretamente. A dança (`extra7`) ficou fora da lista padrão de gestos porque ela gira o corpo livremente, o que quebraria o visual só de frente, lado e costas. Ela pode ser adicionada em `emotes` no `config.json`.

**Limitações atuais:**
- Ela só aparece no monitor principal. Você tem dois; o campo `display` no `config.json` escolhe qual usar (0, 1…).
- Ela é sempre desenhada por cima de tudo. Por isso, em pontos onde uma janela de cima encosta no topo de outra, o corpo dela pode sobrepor a janela de cima.

**Configuração (`config.json`):**
- `petHeight`: tamanho dela em pixels (padrão 110).
- Velocidades de andar, correr e escalar.
- `climbChance`: chance de escalar ao passar por uma lateral.
- `actionWeights`: frequência de cada ação, ou seja, a "personalidade" dela.
- `debug: true`: desenha em verde as superfícies onde ela pode andar e em laranja as laterais que pode escalar.

O código fica em `src/`. Também deixei duas ferramentas de teste: `npm run preview`, que renderiza as animações em PNG na pasta `preview/`, e `npm run sim`, que roda a simulação de comportamento.

Vou finalizar resumindo em português: como executar, configuração, limitações (exibido apenas no monitor principal, o pet fica sempre por cima das janelas sem interação direta, e a única forma de fechar é pelo ícone da bandeja com a opção "Sair" — o que contradiz um pouco a ideia de "sem menus"). O extra7 fica de fora, e vou incluir uma captura de tela.

## 🧑 Você — Wednesday, Sep 30, 2026, 12:18 AM (UTC-3)

tem alguma outra linguagem melhor que essa electron?

### 🤖 Agente

Electron é um framework, não uma linguagem: por baixo, o app é JavaScript rodando num Chromium embutido. Para este pet, o ponto fraco dele é o peso. O executável tem 97 MB e o app deve usar algo como 150–250 MB de RAM (estimativa típica de Electron; não medi neste app), só para desenhar um bonequinho. Na parte técnica ele funciona bem: janela transparente, clique atravessando a janela e 3D com three.js.

Há opções mais leves. Todas funcionam no Windows:

| Opção | Linguagem | Tamanho / RAM | Esforço para migrar |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Tauri** | Rust + o mesmo JS | ~10 MB / ~60–100 MB | Baixo |
| **C# (.NET + WPF ou OpenGL)** | C# | ~1–70 MB / ~50–80 MB | Médio |
| **Godot** | GDScript ou C# | ~70 MB / ~80 MB | Médio |
| **C++ (raylib ou DirectX)** | C++ | ~2 MB / ~20–40 MB | Alto |

- **Tauri** é a melhor troca para este projeto. Ele usa o navegador que já vem no Windows (WebView2) em vez de embutir um Chromium próprio. Toda a parte visual e de comportamento (modelo, animações, lógica da Kurumi) continuaria igual. Só a parte que lê as janelas do Windows seria reescrita em Rust, cerca de 200 linhas.
- **C#** é bom se você quiser um app 100% nativo e fácil de manter no Windows. O acesso às APIs de janela é bem natural. Mas eu teria que reescrever o carregamento do modelo YSM e a renderização.
- **Godot** é um motor de jogo. Faz sentido se você pretende crescer para física, partículas, vários pets ou interações mais complexas. Ele já suporta janela transparente e clique atravessando. Mas o carregador do formato YSM teria que ser reescrito.
- **C++** é o mais leve possível, mas dá muito mais trabalho, e o ganho sobre Tauri ou C# é pequeno para um pet.

Minha recomendação: se o incômodo é o tamanho e o consumo de memória, migrar para **Tauri** reduz o executável de 97 MB para cerca de 10 MB e aproveita quase todo o código. Se está funcionando bem para você, dá para continuar no Electron por enquanto e migrar mais tarde, quando começar a parte de interações e menus.

Quer que eu faça a migração para Tauri?

Electron is actually a framework, not a language, so I should point out real alternatives — Tauri with Rust and a webview for a small footprint that can reuse existing JS/three.js code and supports transparent, click-through windows plus Win32 access; C#/.NET with WPF or WinForms paired with OpenTK for native rendering; or C++ with raylib or DirectX for maximum performance.

Weighing tradeoffs, Electron's downside is its large size and RAM usage but it's already working, so Tauri seems like the best recommendation since most of the renderer code carries over and only the native shell needs rewriting in a small amount of Rust; I'll suggest this while keeping the answer concise and in Portuguese, and ask if they want to migrate.

**O que mudou por dentro:**
- **Continua igual:** a parte do modelo, das animações e do comportamento, sem mudar a lógica.

- **Escala da tela:** sua tela usa escala de 108%, e nessa configuração o Windows informa a escala errada para o Rust. A Kurumi era desenhada fora da tela. Agora a conversão é feita pelo próprio WebView, que sempre sabe a escala certa.
- **Direção inicial:** existia um bug antigo, já presente na versão Electron, em que ela podia começar virada de costas quando deveria estar de frente. Corrigido.

**Comandos do projeto:**
- `npm start`: roda em modo de desenvolvimento.
- `npm run dist`: gera o executável em `dist\Desktop Pet\`.
- `npm run preview`: renderiza as animações em PNG.
- `npm run sim`: roda a simulação de comportamento, que continua passando sem erros.

I'm thinking a Rust polling loop (maybe every 16ms) grabs cursor position and sends it to the renderer, which does hit-testing against the pet's bounding box or alpha pixel, then calls an invoke to toggle interactivity when the state changes. During drag, I want to keep interactivity on even if the cursor leaves the bbox, and when a context menu is open, the whole overlay should capture clicks so clicking outside closes the menu—consuming that click is acceptable. I also need to consider that the overlay isn't focused by default, so clicking on it will activate the window, which could affect z-order detection for other things.

Now I'm designing the right-click menu: a grid-style layout with category tiles like Controle (Parar/Continuar/Começar/Opções) and Modelo (Importar YSM, Ajustar tamanho, Mudar nome), rendered as an HTML panel near the pet with a dark, clean style—clicking a category tile reveals its items in a sub-grid of buttons. For importing YSM files I'll need a file picker via tauri-plugin-dialog, keeping in mind the .ysm format is encrypted.

I'm mapping out the import flow: since .ysm files are encrypted and can't be parsed directly, I'll support importing from an extracted folder or zip archive instead, with the Rust backend reading the files via the zip crate and returning model info (name, animation count, bones, cubes, texture size, metadata) to show in an import preview window with a live rotating model preview, editable name/size fields, and Importar/Cancelar buttons that on confirm copy the model into the models directory and reload the pet with the new config.

For resizing the pet, I'm leaning toward implementing live resizing rather than a full page reload — recomputing pixels-per-unit, canvas dimensions, camera, and behavior scale values through a Pet.applyConfig method, so position state isn't lost. Name changes just get saved to config.json via a merged save_config command, and I still need to fix the floating issue with sit/sleep animations where the root position doesn't align properly with the feet line in different view angles.

For the grounding fix, I'm thinking of switching to precise bounding-box calculation (using actual vertices rather than rotated geometry boxes) since that's what's causing sit and lie poses to float — the overestimated bbox pushes the anchor point too high. I also want to add a "sit" pose config with proper grounding enabled.

Now I'm working through the grab-and-throw mechanic: tracking cursor velocity while the pet is held, adding a pendulum-style tilt based on horizontal movement for physics feel, then releasing with throw velocity and gravity on mouse-up. For interactivity toggling, I'm planning to do pixel-alpha hit testing via readPixels on the WebGL canvas when the cursor is near the pet, switching the window between click-through and interactive modes accordingly, and using pointer capture so drag events continue even if the cursor leaves the window bounds.

I also want to add a mood/emoticon system — tracking energy and happiness values that decay or recover over time, then mapping action states like sleep, grab, throw-landing, or sitting to emoticons (Zzz, surprised, content, dizzy, music note) that pop up above the pet's head, so I should check the available emoticon asset files next.

J'ai ensuite arrêté de tester sur ton bureau et vérifié l'interface dans Edge headless, invisible à l'écran, avec une page de test.

**Ce qui a été fait :**
- **Menu au clic droit sur le pet :** l'en-tête montre le nom, l'état actuel et des barres d'énergie et d'humeur. Il y a trois onglets en grille :
  - **Controle :** Parar, Continuar, Começar (repart du haut de l'écran) et Opções.
  - **Modelo :** Importar modelo, Ajustar tamanho (curseur en direct), Mudar nome, plus les infos du modèle actuel.
  - **Opções :** taille, vitesse, probabilité d'escalader les fenêtres, emoticons activés ou non, leur fréquence, et mode debug.

  Tout est sauvegardé dans `config.json`.
- **Importation :** tu choisis une pasta ou un `.zip`. Un aperçu 3D animé s'affiche, avec les boutons Frente/Direita/Esquerda/Costas et un clic sur chaque animation pour la voir. On y voit :
  - le nombre d'animations et leur répartition par fichier ;
  - les os, les cubes et les textures (avec choix de la texture) ;
  - les auteurs, et un avertissement s'il manque une animation dont le pet a besoin.

  Tu peux aussi définir le nom et la taille. Le modèle est copié dans `models\` puis chargé.
- **Assise et couchée :** le modèle ne flotte plus. Il est posé au sol selon son point le plus bas, avec une transition douce. Je l'ai vérifié sur la planche d'aperçu, dans toutes les directions.
- **Attraper :**
  - Clic gauche maintenu : le pet suit la souris en se balançant comme un pendule.
  - En relâchant, il est lancé avec l'élan du geste et rebondit sur les bords de l'écran.
  - Un lancer fort la laisse étourdie à l'atterrissage.
  - Un clic court est une caresse : l'humeur monte et un cœur apparaît.
- **Emoticons :** les GIFs du dossier `emoticons` apparaissent au-dessus de la tête selon l'action ou l'humeur. Par exemple :
  - surprise quand on l'attrape, et « help » si on la tient plus de 5 secondes ;
  - des points de suspension en dormant ;
  - un bâillement quand l'énergie est basse, tristesse ou colère quand l'humeur baisse.

  L'énergie diminue avec le temps et remonte quand elle dort.

Le reste du bureau reste cliquable normalement : la fenêtre du pet ne capte la souris que quand le curseur est sur un pixel visible du pet, ou quand le menu ou la fenêtre d'importation est ouvert. Je l'ai vérifié sur le vrai bureau. La simulation de 30 minutes, qui couvre maintenant pause, reprise, saisie, lancer, caresse et changement de taille, passe sans erreur.

**Limite :** les fichiers `.ysm` sont chiffrés par le mod et ne peuvent pas être lus. En choisir un affiche un message qui l'explique. Il faut importer la version exportée du modèle : un dossier contenant `main.json`, les fichiers `*.animation.json` et la texture `.png`, ou un `.zip` de ce dossier.

Les fichiers nouveaux ou modifiés côté interface sont `ui.js`, `emoticons.js`, `style.css`, `pet.js`, `behavior.js` et `view.js`. Côté Rust, ce sont `main.rs`, le nouveau `model_io.rs` et `win32.rs`. La page de test est `test/ui.html`.

O que foi feito:

**O app já está pronto e rodando:** `dist\Desktop Pet\Desktop Pet.exe`.

**Um problema durante os testes, que você precisa conferir:** testei o menu simulando cliques no seu desktop real, e dois cliques acertaram o seu navegador.
- O primeiro abriu o menu de clique direito do navegador. Esse eu fechei.
- O segundo clicou no seu favorito "Facebook", e a aba da Twitch saiu da live.
- A ferramenta de segurança bloqueou minha tentativa de voltar a página. Use o botão Voltar do navegador para retornar.

Depois disso, parei de testar no seu desktop e testei a interface num navegador invisível, separado.

**O que foi implementado:**
- **Menu no clique direito sobre o pet:**
  - **Cabeçalho:** nome, o que ela está fazendo e barras de Energia e Humor.
  - **Controle:** Parar, Continuar, Começar (recomeça do topo da tela) e Opções.
  - **Modelo:** Importar modelo, Ajustar tamanho (com controle deslizante que muda na hora), Mudar nome e informações do modelo atual.
  - **Opções:** tamanho, velocidade, chance de escalar janelas, liga/desliga dos emoticons, frequência dos emoticons e modo debug.
  - Tudo fica salvo no `config.json`.
- **Importar modelo:**
  - Você escolhe a pasta ou um `.zip` do modelo.
  - Aparece uma prévia 3D animada, com botões Frente/Direita/Esquerda/Costas. Clicando no nome de uma animação, ela toca na prévia.
  - A janela mostra quantas animações o modelo tem, ossos, cubos, texturas (dá para escolher qual usar) e autores. Também avisa se faltar alguma animação que o pet usa.
  - Você define o nome e o tamanho antes de importar.
- **Sentar e deitar:** o modelo não flutua mais, fica apoiado no chão. Conferi em todas as direções.
- **Agarrar com o mouse:**
  - Clique esquerdo e segure: o pet segue o mouse balançando como um pêndulo.
  - Ao soltar, ele é arremessado com o embalo do movimento e quica nas bordas da tela.
  - Se o arremesso for forte, ela cai tonta.
  - Um clique rápido é um carinho: o humor sobe e aparece um coração.
- **Emoticons acima da cabeça**, de acordo com a ação ou o humor:
  - Susto ao ser agarrada, e "help" se ficar presa mais de 5 segundos.
  - Reticências enquanto dorme.
  - Bocejo com pouca energia, e tristeza ou raiva com o humor baixo.
  - A energia cai com o tempo e volta quando ela dorme.
- **O resto do desktop continua clicável:** a janela do pet só captura o mouse quando o cursor está em cima dela, ou com o menu ou a importação abertos.

**Limitação:** arquivos `.ysm` são criptografados pelo mod e não podem ser lidos. Para importar, use a versão exportada do modelo: a pasta com `main.json`, os arquivos de animação e a textura `.png`, ou um `.zip` dessa pasta.

### 🤖 Agente

Pedido grande. Vou começar conferindo os nomes dos ossos do braço no modelo, para fazer o pet ser segurado pela mão.

This is a big request, so let me plan each piece. For grabbing by the arm, I'm thinking the pet's position should snap so its hand/arm bone sits at the cursor, with the arm rotated upward to look like it's being held — overriding the normal animation for that visual effect.

To find the actual hand position, I'd rotate the RightArm bone upward then compute its bounding box so the topmost point gives the hand tip location, which I can convert into pixel offsets relative to the pet's feet anchor. That hand point becomes the pendulum pivot, and since the model faces the camera, it doesn't matter which arm visually ends up on which side. I'm figuring out the right rotation value to make the arm point straight up in the animation rig.

On grab, I'll call setHold(true) and measure the hand position via handOffset, then compute the offset so the pet's hand snaps to the cursor, smoothing the transition over about 0.15 seconds. I'll stub these view methods in the simulation harness with a fixed hand position, and handle release by clearing the hold state, then work out the pendulum length from the hand-to-center-of-mass distance when the arm is raised.

Next I need to add config options for emoticon scale and offset with sliders in the options menu, move the animation preview chips to sit directly under the model in the import modal since users complained the list is too far down to see animations play, and add four status bars (Saúde, Fome, Humor, Energia) to the menu header along with action buttons like Cafuné, Dar tapa, and Alimentar that pull food from inventory.

For the sleep and play actions, I'm planning sleep to boost energy while draining hunger over time, and a 60-second mouse-chase minigame for Brincar where the pet runs toward the cursor and jumps to reach it using ballistic physics, landing naturally on surfaces via the existing air-landing logic, with a catch triggering a humor bonus and happy emoticon.

After landing, I need the chase to persist via a state flag so the pet resumes chasing instead of going idle, with catch detection also checked while airborne, and the chase window counting down with an optional HUD timer badge above the pet, ending with a chaseEnd event reporting whether the catch succeeded. For movement, I'm tuning run speed, jump trigger conditions based on cursor position and reach, jump cooldown, and edge-of-surface behavior where the pet hops off toward the cursor if it's beyond the current platform.

Pausing or grabbing the pet during a chase cancels it without bonus, and now I'm shifting to designing status decay rates for hunger, mood, and energy over time, including how low hunger drains health and how difficulty settings (Fácil, Normal, Difícil, Extremo) scale these rates as multipliers.

I'm deciding against offline decay for simplicity, planning periodic save-file persistence of stats and inventory, and defining death behavior: health hitting zero triggers a death animation, resets all progress, and shows a "Começar de novo" modal. For the shop, I'll keep foods free of charge, add a short list of generic drinks alongside the foods, and leave Mobília as "Em breve" per the user's request.

Tudo implementado e o app novo já está rodando (`dist\Desktop Pet\Desktop Pet.exe`). Desta vez testei sem mexer no seu desktop: usei a simulação automática do comportamento, que passou sem erros em 30 e 60 minutos, e capturas de tela num navegador invisível.

**Mudanças que você pediu:**
- **Segurar pelo braço:** ao agarrar, o pet levanta o braço direito e fica pendurado pela mão embaixo do cursor, balançando.
- **Emoticons:** em Opções → Emoticons dá para ajustar o **Tamanho**, a **Posição X** e a **Posição Y**. Ao mexer, aparece um emoticon de exemplo para você ver o resultado na hora.
- **Importar modelo:** a lista de animações agora fica logo abaixo da prévia do modelo. Clicando numa animação, você a vê tocar sem precisar rolar a janela.

**Atributos** (mostrados no topo do menu, atualizando ao vivo): **Saúde, Fome, Humor e Energia**.
- **Queda gradual:** Fome, Humor e Energia caem com o tempo. Na dificuldade Normal, a Fome vai de cheia a vazia em cerca de 2h30.
- **Fome:** quanto maior, melhor. Barra vazia significa estômago vazio.
- **Saúde:** com a Fome abaixo de 50%, a Saúde começa a cair, e quanto mais vazio o estômago, mais rápido. Com a Fome acima de 70%, a Saúde se recupera aos poucos.
- **Saúde em zero:** o pet morre, aparece a janela "Você perdeu seu pet" e todo o progresso (atributos e inventário) é apagado. O botão "Começar de novo" recomeça do zero.
- **Salvamento:** o progresso é salvo a cada 15 segundos no arquivo `save.json`, ao lado do executável.

**Novo menu (6 categorias): Ações, Controle, Shop, Inventário, Modelo e Opções.**
- **Ações:**
  - Cafuné: +Humor.
  - Dar tapa: −Humor e −Saúde.
  - Alimentar: escolhe uma comida do inventário. Se não houver nenhuma, aparece o botão "Ir para a Shop".
  - Colocar para dormir: dorme pelo menos 20 segundos; durante o sono a Energia sobe e a Fome cai.
  - Brincar: começa o pega-pega descrito abaixo.
- **Pega-pega (Brincar):**
  - Por 60 segundos, o pet corre e pula atrás do seu mouse.
  - Um contador aparece perto dele.
  - Se ele conseguir pegar o mouse, ganha um bônus de Humor.
  - Brincar gasta Fome e Energia. Com a Energia muito baixa, ela se recusa e boceja.
- **Shop:** abas Comidas, Bebidas e Mobília. Fiz 15 comidas genéricas e, como a categoria Bebidas existe, também 6 bebidas. Todas são grátis. Mobília mostra "em breve".
- **Inventário:** mostra tudo o que você tem e a quantidade, com o botão "Usar".
- **Opções → Dificuldade:** Fácil, Normal, Difícil e Extremo, com a queda 0,5×, 1×, 2× e 4× mais rápida. Pode ser trocada a qualquer hora.

O pet também reage com emoticons ao tapa, à comida, ao pega-pega e à fome ou saúde baixas.

**Uma decisão que tomei por conta própria:** os atributos **não caem com o app fechado**. Quando você abre de novo, eles continuam de onde pararam. Se quiser que o tempo fechado também conte, me avise.

For the QTE chase mechanic, when the cursor gets caught the pet pauses in a grabbing animation while a progress bar and joystick UI appears prompting alternating key presses, which requires briefly stealing window focus from the user's active app so the overlay can capture keyboard input. I'm designing the scoring so alternating arrow presses count fully while repeated same-key presses only give partial credit, with the progress bar continuously draining at a rate tied to the pet's health stat.

I'm working through the math for how fast the bar drains versus how much each keypress fills it — tuning constants so that at full health the player needs roughly 5-6 presses per second to fill the bar within the time limit, while lower health makes it noticeably easier, matching the idea that "higher health, higher difficulty." I'm also puzzling over what winning the QTE actually means narratively — whether filling the bar represents the pet successfully catching the cursor or the user successfully fending it off, since the original request was ambiguous on that point.

Pressing keys fills the bar toward escape; reaching full triggers a stun/dizzy animation before the pet resumes chasing, and I need a brief grace period after escaping so the cursor isn't immediately re-caught. I'm treating the QTE as a ground-style action with no movement, and handling the tricky case where the cursor gets caught mid-air during a jump — letting the QTE start in air but deferring the hold-state transition until landing.

**Comportamento**
- **Sono:** o pet não dorme sozinho com Energia acima de 70%. No menu, "Colocar para dormir" fica desativado e mostra "Sem sono (Energia acima de 70%)".
- **Emoticons:** com os 4 status em 70% ou mais, os negativos (triste, bravo, socorro, fome, sono, tonto) não aparecem. Quando a reação é obrigatória, como levar um tapa, aparece um de surpresa no lugar.
- **Carregar:** clicando à esquerda do pet ele levanta o braço esquerdo; à direita, o direito. Conferi os dois lados nas capturas. O balanço logo depois de pegar ficou mais suave.

**Menu**
- **Transparência:** se o pet estiver atrás do menu, ele fica com 30% de opacidade. Volta ao normal quando você passa o mouse por cima, para conseguir clicar.
- **Botão direito segurado:** o pedido ficou ambíguo, então fiz as duas coisas. Arrastar sobre o menu move o menu; arrastar sobre o pet carrega o pet. Nos dois casos o menu continua aberto.
- **Mudança sua:** clicar no pet com o menu aberto não fecha mais o menu.

**Pega-pega com quick-time event**
Eu entendi assim, pela regra "Saúde alta = mais difícil": quando o pet pega o mouse, é você quem tenta escapar.
- Aparece um painel com o Joystick, as setas ← → e uma barra.
- Apertar as setas alternando enche a barra; repetir a mesma seta quase não conta.
- O pet puxa a barra para baixo mais rápido quanto maior a Saúde dele.
- **Se você escapar:** o pet fica atordoado um instante e a perseguição continua.
- **Se a barra zerar ou o tempo de 8 s acabar:** o pet vence e ganha +20 de Humor.
- As setas são lidas direto pelo Windows enquanto o painel está na tela, porque o Windows não deixa o pet tomar o foco. Por isso elas também chegam ao programa que estiver em primeiro plano.

Se a ideia era a contrária (encher a barra para o pet vencer), me avise que eu inverto.

**Sons**
Todos os sons podem ser ligados/desligados e têm volume em Opções → Som.
- **Collide:** bater na borda da tela, quedas fortes e tapa, sorteando as variações.
- **Player:** passos ao andar, correr e escalar, além de pulo, aterrissagem, arremesso e o teleporte do "Começar".
- **UI:** abrir e fechar o menu, cliques, abas, interruptores e o arrastar dos controles deslizantes.
- **Comer e beber:** sons 8-bit gerados pelo app — mastigar e engolir para comida, goles e um "ahh" para bebida.

**Comer**
- Não é mais instantâneo: o pet segura a comida com as duas mãos, leva à boca e dá 4 mordidas, e a comida vai sumindo a cada uma. Bebida são 3 goles, inclinando o copo.
- Os status sobem a cada mordida.
- Dá para pedir até 5 itens; ele come um de cada vez.
- O pet come em pé. Sentado, a animação inclinava o corpo e a comida ia parar acima da cabeça.

**Shop**
- Agora tem 27 itens com os sprites do pack: 21 comidas japonesas e 6 matchas.
- O pack só tem bebidas de matcha, então as outras bebidas saíram.
- Itens antigos que ainda estiverem no inventário continuam podendo ser usados, mas não aparecem mais na Shop.

O executável passou de 4,2 para 5,4 MB, porque os sons e imagens agora vão dentro dele.

Seu pet está com Fome em 41%. Abaixo de 50% a Saúde começa a cair, então vale alimentá-lo logo.

Preciso montar um resumo em pt-BR cobrindo as interpretações: a semântica do QTE (usuário tenta escapar; se falhar o pet ganha +20 de humor; ao escapar o pet fica tonto e a perseguição continua), o arrastar com botão direito tanto no menu quanto no pet, os emoticons negativos substituídos por surpresa quando forçado, os itens legados, o catálogo de comidas, as setas lidas globalmente só durante o QTE (mas também chegando ao app focado), a impossibilidade de verificar sons por estar headless, e o comer em pé. Agora vou revisar as estatísticas atuais.

With around 30 features to implement, I need to be pragmatic—writing larger chunks of code with lighter verification like a sim run, one build, and a few screenshots. I'm starting to go through each feature and decide on an implementation approach, beginning with generating pixel art for a cafuné hand animation over the head.

For the anti-spam pat detection, I'm planning to track pat timestamps and trigger an angry mood drop if too many happen in a short window. For deleting saves, I'll add a confirmation-gated reset button in the profile controls that wipes stats and revives a fresh pet. For shop purchases, I'm weighing quantity selectors (1/5/10) versus a stepper, while keeping single-copy toys simple. The multi-monitor drag feature is the trickiest—it would need the overlay window to span a virtual bounding box across selected monitors' work areas, which is a substantial Rust-side change.

Rather than spanning monitors (which breaks with mixed DPI), I'm leaning toward a simpler "monitor switching" approach: when the pet walks or is dragged off the edge of the current monitor into an adjacent allowed monitor, the overlay switches displays and repositions the pet at the corresponding edge. This would need a `set_display(index)` command and monitor metadata in the world event, plus edge-detection logic comparing cursor position against the current monitor's work area during drag.

For dragging specifically, I'll detect when the cursor leaves the current monitor's bounds, switch the display, and drop the pet at the cursor's x position on the new monitor rather than trying to preserve pointer capture across the switch. For walking, I'll add a 'screenEdge' event fired when the pet hits the floor's screen boundary, which checks for an allowed neighboring monitor and triggers the same switch-and-reposition logic, spawning the pet walking in from the opposite edge. I also need a monitor-selection UI with checkboxes so users can choose which detected monitors are allowed.

I need to check the Rust side for how display config is currently parsed (it's just a "primary" string right now) and add a proper index-based system, plus commands to set the active display and emit the monitor list with the world state.

For the medicine category, I'm planning to use emoji icons for pills, bandages, and potions rather than generating pixel art, with remedies giving instant health, vitamins giving health over time, and powerups granting temporary buffs like paused hunger drain, speed boosts, or doubled mood — tracked via a simple buffs map with countdown timers on the pet, reusing the eating animation for drinking.

For jam mode reacting to music, I'm weighing options: WebView2's getDisplayMedia could capture system audio but requires a user prompt, whereas a proper Rust-side WASAPI loopback capture (possibly via the cpal crate, which supports Windows loopback input) would let me compute audio energy and beats natively and stream that data to the renderer without interruption.

I'm now working out how to use that cpal loopback capture — building an input stream from the output device to grab system audio, then computing RMS levels and a rough beat detector using energy spikes against a moving average with a basic low-pass filter, emitting small audio packets every ~30ms with level, bass, and beat flags. For the "Assistir" watching mode I'm thinking through jumpscare detection (sudden loudness spikes) and acknowledging that true laughter/comedy detection isn't really feasible — I'll implement a rough heuristic based on sustained energy variance and be upfront that it's approximate, accepting the extra compile time cpal adds as a reasonable tradeoff; for jam mode's dancing, I'll tie it into the existing extra-animation mapping system for "dance" animations.

For smoother turning, I'm planning to replace the current snap-to-yaw facing logic with a lerp toward the target yaw over about a quarter second along the shortest rotational path, rather than instantly jumping between facing directions. For Assistir mode, the pet should detect the active video window and sit facing "back" toward the screen as if watching it, though I need to handle the case where the canvas currently hides itself during fullscreen video playback, which would break this behavior.

For jumpscares and comedic beats I'll trigger fear or happy reactions with matching emoticons and jump animations, and for eating during watch mode I'll just reuse the standing eating animation at a slower pace rather than reworking the sitting pose, then return to the watching sit afterward. I still need to decide exactly where the pet positions itself -- likely walking to the floor position below the center of whichever window is playing the video.

Actually, simplifying further: skip the walking logic entirely and just have the pet sit in its current spot facing backward. I'm also rethinking health regen to only occur when both mood and hunger exceed 80%, and planning out profile data fields like birth date, items consumed, pats, slaps, chases, and wins for a new Profile button. I'm sketching out theme options (pixel art, retro, synthwave, various Game Boy palettes) implemented through CSS variable overrides tied to a data-theme attribute, which means expanding the menu grid to 3x3 to fit new categories like Profile, Themes, and Play modes. For the shop, I'm outlining a category of "fun stuff" items that get placed on the desktop from inventory, where the pet interacts with them automatically to gradually boost stats based on difficulty, with only one copy allowed per item and some wear-and-tear mechanic over time.

Since items would need prices and there's no currency system yet, I'm introducing a coin economy: starting coins, passive earning while the pet is happy, and bonus coins from games, while keeping food free and reserving prices for fun stuff and medicine, with a coin display in the header. I'm also designing an "unboxing" sequence for purchased items where the pet walks over, examines it with curiosity, then stores it in inventory with a mood bonus, and similarly planning a Game Boy feature where the pet holds and plays it with chiptune music (starting with the Tetris theme) and a tiny on-screen mini-game rendered on canvas, requiring me to crop the asset image down to just the front-facing sprite.

I'm extending this with a cartridge system tied to the Game Boy, where buying one unlocks a cartridge shop, the pet stores cartridges in a bag object, and the pet randomly picks a game to play with its own simplified mini-simulation and matching sound effects for titles like Pong, Asteroids, Space Invaders, and Galaga. I'm also refining smaller details: fixing emote positioning when hitting the window's top edge, adding straw-and-sip animations for drinking instead of tilting, and correcting how sitting/lying ground level is calculated so hair and accessories don't incorrectly lower the floor position -- which means filtering by bone names in the model structure instead of using the full bounding box.

I'm also planning animation role assignments during model import so users can map extra imported animations to actions like dancing, celebrating, or waving, plus chase mechanics like a 3-2-1 countdown, smarter floor-dropping behavior, capped jump heights with wall-grabbing, wall-jumps and long leaps during chases, and a hiding mechanic using clip-path to make the pet appear to duck behind windows. ### 🤖 Agente

Os 31 itens da lista estão implementados, o executável novo foi gerado e já está aberto em `dist\Desktop Pet\Desktop Pet.exe`. A simulação de comportamento rodou 30 e 60 minutos sem erros, e o app iniciou e gravou o save no formato novo. Os testes não cobriram dois monitores reais nem a reação a áudio real, e as alterações feitas depois das capturas (posição da mão do cafuné e do Game Boy, abas da loja) só foram conferidas no código.

**Menu (agora em grade 3×3)**
- **Moedas:** você começa com 300. Ganha 1 por minuto, 15 quando o pet vence o pega-pega e 10 por pedido atendido. Comidas e bebidas continuam grátis.
- **Shop:** permite comprar ×1, ×5 ou ×10 (menos mobília e brinquedos).
- **Medicina:** vitamina, curativo, remédio, poção e PowerUps temporários (sem fome, humor em dobro, sem cansaço, escudo de saúde). Os PowerUps ativos aparecem no topo do menu.
- **Fun stuffs:** Game Boy, pelúcia e bola. Vão para o Inventário e você usa "Colocar" para pôr na área de trabalho.
- **Perfil:** nome, idade, moedas, itens consumidos e favoritos, cafunés, pega-pega, jogos, pedidos. Tem também o botão de apagar o save (pede um segundo clique para confirmar).
- **Temas:** Padrão, Pixel art, Retrô, Synthwave, Game Boy, Game Boy Light e Game Boy Color.
- **Opções:**
  - volume e tom da voz 8-bit (grave, normal, agudo);
  - escolha de monitores, que só aparece com 2 ou mais.

**Brinquedos e Game Boy**
- **Na compra:** o pet fica animado, vai até o item, pega e examina, e ganha humor.
- **Uso:** o pet brinca sozinho. O ganho de humor é gradual e menor nas dificuldades mais altas. Só existe um de cada na área de trabalho.
- **Desgaste:** quebra depois de 20 a 50 usos. Clicar no brinquedo quebrado conserta pela metade do preço. Se ficar quebrado, o pet fica triste e perde humor.
- **Game Boy:** o pet segura o aparelho e balança um pouco de um lado para o outro. Acima dele aparece uma tela com o jogo rodando e som 8-bit. Tetris vem de fábrica, com a música do Tetris.
- **Cartuchos:** depois que o Game Boy é comprado, ele some da loja e aparecem Galaga, Pong, Asteroids e Space Invaders. A bolsa do Game Boy surge na primeira compra; clique nela para escolher o jogo.

**Brincar**
- **Pega-pega:**
  - contagem 3-2-1 antes de começar;
  - um sino preso ao cursor que balança e toca;
  - o pet dá saltos longos, pula nas paredes e se esconde atrás de janelas para dar o bote.
- **Jam:** o pet dança a música que está tocando no PC, no ritmo das batidas, com notas musicais coloridas que brilham em cada batida.
- **Assistir:** o pet senta de costas, olhando para o vídeo, e continua visível mesmo em tela cheia. Leva susto com sons altos repentinos e se anima com risadas. Você pode dar comida e bebida enquanto ele assiste, e ele come mais devagar.

**Comportamento**
- **Cafuné:** aparece uma mão em pixel art acariciando a cabeça. Mais de 5 cafunés em 10 segundos deixam o pet bravo e ele perde humor.
- **Pedidos:** com Humor abaixo de 60%, o pet pede de vez em quando uma comida, bebida, cafuné ou brincadeira específica. O pedido aparece num balão com ícone em pixel art e vem com a voz 8-bit.
- **Saúde:** só regenera com Humor **e** Fome acima de 80%. O seu pet atual está com Saúde 29% e Humor 37%, então ele não vai se recuperar até o Humor subir.
- **Giro:** passa por todos os ângulos até virar de lado.
- **Sentar e deitar:** o chão agora é medido só pelo corpo, sem cabelo, roupa ou saia.
- **Pulos:** no máximo 25% da altura do monitor. O pet pode pular e se agarrar no meio da lateral de uma janela, e sabe descer pulando para um andar mais baixo.
- **Emoticon:** aparece abaixo da cabeça quando encosta no topo da tela.
- **Bebida:** não inclina mais, às vezes tem canudo, e o gole é contínuo.
- **Animações extras:** ao importar um modelo, o app pergunta para que serve cada uma (Emote, Dança, Comemorar, Medo, Rir). Também dá para editar depois em Modelo → Animações extras.

**Limitações**
- O susto e a risada no Assistir são estimados pelo som. Vão errar às vezes.
- O pet troca de monitor de dois jeitos: quando você o carrega até a borda da tela ou quando ele anda até a borda. Nos dois casos a camada do pet inteira muda para o outro monitor; ele não aparece nos dois ao mesmo tempo.

![Game Boy na tela](J:\meus app\Desktop Pets\preview\ui\gameboy.png)

I should also flag that the user's existing pet has health 29 and mood 37 — under the new regen rule requiring both stats above 80, its health won't recover unless its mood improves too.

**Coisas que só aparecem em certas condições**
- **Mão do cafuné:** clique rápido no pet ou use Ações → Cafuné.
- **Pet bravo:** dê mais de 5 cafunés em 10 segundos.

- **Brinquedos:**
  - compre, depois vá em **Inventário → Colocar**;
  - o pet vai até o brinquedo sozinho, em momentos aleatórios, e não brinca com o mesmo brinquedo de novo em menos de 45 segundos;
  - quebram só depois de 20 a 50 usos.
- **Cartuchos e bolsa:** só aparecem na loja depois de comprar o Game Boy. A bolsa surge depois do primeiro cartucho.
- **Curiosidade ao comprar:** o pet precisa estar no chão, sem estar comendo ou brincando.
- **Sino no cursor, saltos longos, pulo na parede e esconder atrás da janela:** só durante o pega-pega. Esconder exige uma janela não maximizada perto do pet.
- **Regeneração de Saúde:** só com Humor **e** Fome acima de 80%.
- **Emoticon embaixo da cabeça:** só quando o pet está no topo da tela.
- **Canudo:** aparece em cerca de metade das bebidas.
- **Pular até 25% da altura da tela, agarrar no meio da janela e pular para o andar de baixo:** acontecem aleatoriamente enquanto o pet passeia.
- **Giro suave:** a cada mudança de direção.
- **Sentar e deitar sem flutuar:** ao sentar ou dormir.
- **Assistir em tela cheia:** o pet não some mais na tela cheia. Se o player cobrir a janela do pet, isso depende do Windows.

**Para conferir se é a versão nova**
Feche o pet pela bandeja e abra `J:\meus app\Desktop Pets\dist\Desktop Pet\Desktop Pet.exe`. O menu deve ter 9 abas: Ações, Brincar, Controle, Shop, Inventário, Perfil, Modelo, Temas, Opções. Se aparecerem só 6, é um executável antigo, por exemplo um atalho apontando para outro lugar.

Os cinco itens já estavam no app, e testei cada um com cliques simulados; o que faltava era jeito de chegar até eles. Eram dois problemas, que corrigi; o executável novo foi gerado e já está aberto:

- **Moedas:** com as 300 iniciais, o Game Boy (250) deixava só 50 e o cartucho custava 60. Por isso não dava para chegar aos cartuchos nem à bolsa.
  - Game Boy agora custa **180**.
  - Cartucho agora custa **30**.
  - Você passa a ganhar **2 moedas por minuto** (antes era 1).
- **Pet demorava para brincar:** ele só ia até o Game Boy quando "dava vontade", e só se já estivesse no chão. Agora, ao colocar o brinquedo, ele vai na hora; se estiver em cima de uma janela, pula para o chão antes.

Pequeno ajuste: com canudo, o copo fica um pouco mais baixo para o canudo chegar à boca, em vez de o copo cobrir o rosto.

**Como ver cada item**
1. **Game Boy:**
   - **Shop** → aba **Fun stuffs** → Game Boy;
   - depois **Inventário** → **Colocar**;
   - o pet vai até ele, joga Tetris com a tela verde acima da cabeça e som 8-bit, e o Humor sobe aos poucos.
2. **Cartuchos:**
   - depois de comprar o Game Boy, ele some da loja e aparecem Galaga, Pong, Asteroids e Space Invaders;
   - na primeira compra surge a **bolsa** ao lado do Game Boy;
   - clique nela para ver e escolher o cartucho.
3. **Emote embaixo da cabeça:** carregue o pet até o topo da tela; o balão passa para baixo da cabeça.
4. **Bebida:** fica em pé na boca, num gole contínuo, e em cerca de metade das vezes tem canudo.

Toda compra (até comida e bebida) faz o pet ir até o item, pegar e examinar por uns 4 segundos antes de usar. Se você comprar uma bebida e mandar beber logo em seguida, ele primeiro examina o copo e depois bebe.

- Testei com cliques simulados o fluxo do Game Boy: comprar, colocar pelo Inventário, o pet ir jogar com a tela de Tetris acima dele, comprar um cartucho, a bolsa aparecer e abrir a lista de cartuchos. Tudo funcionou.
- O emote aparece embaixo da cabeça quando o pet encosta no topo da tela.
- A bebida fica em pé na boca, num gole contínuo, e em cerca de metade das vezes tem canudo.

**O que mudei**
- **Preços:** o Game Boy custava 250 e cada cartucho 60. Com as 300 moedas iniciais, não sobrava dinheiro para nenhum cartucho. Agora o Game Boy custa 180, o cartucho 30, e você ganha 2 moedas por minuto (antes era 1).
- **Brincar logo:** ao colocar o Game Boy na área de trabalho, o pet vai jogar na hora. Se estiver em cima de uma janela, pula para o chão antes.
- **Canudo:** o copo fica um pouco mais baixo para o canudo chegar à boca, sem cobrir o rosto.

**Observação:** toda compra faz o pet ir até o item, pegar e examinar por uns 4 segundos. Só depois ele come ou bebe.

O executável novo já está aberto em `dist\Desktop Pet\Desktop Pet.exe`.

I notice some of my progress messages slipped into French — no need to dwell on that, I'll just continue and keep the summary in pt-BR.

## 🧑 Você — 

<available_subagent_types>
Available subagent_types and a quick description of what they do:
- generalPurpose: General-purpose agent for researching complex questions, searching for code, and executing multi-step tasks. Use when searching for a keyword or file and not confident you'll find the match quickly.
- explore: Fast agent specialized for exploring codebases. Use this when you need to quickly find files by patterns (eg. "src/components/**/*.tsx"), search code for keywords (eg. "API endpoints"), or answer questions about the codebase (eg. "how do API endpoints work?"). When calling this agent, specify the desired thoroughness level: "quick" for basic searches, "medium" for moderate exploration, or "very thorough" for comprehensive analysis across multiple locations and naming conventions.
- browser-use: Perform browser-based testing and web automation. This subagent can navigate web pages, interact with elements, fill forms, and take screenshots. Use this for testing web applications, verifying UI changes, or any browser-based tasks. Use this browser subagent when you need to either: (1) parallelize browser tasks alongside other work, or (2) execute a longer sequence of browser actions that benefit from dedicated context. For simple, single browser actions, you may use the browser tools directly. This subagent requires agent mode because browser MCP access is unavailable in readonly mode. This subagent_type is stateful; if a browserUse subagent already exists, the previously created subagent will be resumed if you reuse the Task tool with subagent_type set to browserUse. (Auto-resumes most recent agent of this type; `resume` arg is ignored)
- cursor-guide: Read Cursor product documentation to answer questions about how Cursor Desktop, IDE, CLI, Cloud Agents, Bugbot, and other features work. Use when the user asks 'In Cursor, how do I...?' or similar questions about Cursor products.
- ci-investigator: Investigate a single failing PR CI check and return a short root-cause summary. Use when the user asks to summarize, explain, diagnose, or investigate a specific failed check from a pull request.
- bugbot: Use only when the user *explicitly* asks for a Bugbot-like review of local code changes. When launching this subagent, set the Task description to exactly "Bugbot". Launch exactly one Bugbot subagent with `run_in_background: false` unless the user explicitly asks to run in background. Use this fixed prompt form: "Full Repository Path: ...\nDiff: <one of: \"branch changes\", \"uncommitted changes\", \"natural language\">\nChange Description: ...\nCustom Instructions: ..."; default to `Diff: branch changes`; include `Change Description` only when `Diff` is `natural language`, formatting it as one block per changed file (a `<path> (added|modified|deleted|renamed)` header followed by bullets of what changed, mentioning line numbers or ranges inline where helpful), and only use `natural language` as a last resort after a regular diff-based review failed because the diff could not be computed; include `Custom Instructions` only when the user gave specific review instructions. This subagent is single-shot and does not support `resume`; always launch a fresh subagent instead.
- security-review: Use only when the user *explicitly* asks for a security review of local code changes. When launching this subagent, set the Task description to exactly "Security Review". Launch exactly one security-review subagent with `run_in_background: false` unless the user explicitly asks to run in background. Use this fixed prompt form: "Full Repository Path: ...\nDiff: <one of: \"branch changes\", \"uncommitted changes\">\nCustom Instructions: ..."; default to `Diff: branch changes`; include `Custom Instructions` only when the user gave specific review instructions. This subagent is single-shot and does not support `resume`; always launch a fresh subagent instead.
- best-of-n-runner: Run a task in an isolated git worktree. Each best-of-n-runner gets its own branch and working directory. Use for best-of-N parallel attempts or isolated experiments.
- compatibility-scan-review: Run the agent-compatibility CLI and return the raw repository score with its main problems
- docs-reliability-review: Check whether the documented setup and run paths reliably lead to the real working path
- startup-review: Try to bootstrap and start a repository like a cold agent, then report where the path breaks down
- validation-review: Assess whether an agent can verify a small change without guessing or running an unnecessarily heavy loop
- performance-marketing-agent: Brand-specific performance marketing agent. Use proactively when the user asks about
ad campaigns, campaign performance, budget optimization, keyword research, ad copy,
audience targeting, or anything related to Google Ads, Meta Ads, Amazon Ads, ChatGPT
Ads, LinkedIn Ads, or TikTok Ads. Also use when the user wants to create campaigns,
write ad copy, or analyze advertising data for their brand.

- data-operations: Performs bulk contact operations — mass tagging, list migration, field cleanup, data import. Always previews changes and warns about automation triggers before executing.
- marketing-planner: Creates multi-step marketing plans (30/60/90 day, quarterly calendars, launch plans) grounded in real ActiveCampaign account data. Read-only — produces plans, doesn't execute.
- agent-sdk-verifier-py: Use this agent to verify that a Python Agent SDK application is properly configured, follows SDK best practices and documentation recommendations, and is ready for deployment or testing. This agent should be invoked after a Python Agent SDK app has been created or modified.
- agent-sdk-verifier-ts: Use this agent to verify that a TypeScript Agent SDK application is properly configured, follows SDK best practices and documentation recommendations, and is ready for deployment or testing. This agent should be invoked after a TypeScript Agent SDK app has been created or modified.
- changelog-generator
- claude-ecom: Use this agent when analyzing ecommerce order/sales CSV data to produce consultant-style business reviews. Generates KPI decomposition trees, health signals, prioritized findings, and concrete action plans across multiple time horizons (30d/90d/365d).
Examples:
<example> Context: User has an order-level CSV and wants a full business review User: /ecom review Agent: Runs Python analytics engine on the CSV, interprets the structured output, and writes a REVIEW.md with executive summary, KPI trees per period, findings, and a prioritized action plan. </example>
<example> Context: User wants a focused 90-day analysis User: /ecom review 90d Agent: Produces a period-specific review focused on 90-day momentum with KPI decomposition and growth drivers. </example>

- claude-persona: Use this agent when building AI persona panels and pressure-testing product concepts before paying for fieldwork. Generates diverse synthetic personas, runs agent-separated interviews and concept tests (each persona as an independent `claude -p` subprocess), and produces an executive research report with theme synthesis, cross-tabs, charts, and verbatims. Inspired by Microsoft's TinyTroupe.
Examples:
<example> Context: Marketer wants quick qualitative signal on a new launch idea User: /persona generate 10 Gen Z skincare shoppers in the US Agent: Builds a 10-persona panel with diverse demographics, Big Five traits, and segment balance. Saves the panel for reuse across follow-up studies. </example>
<example> Context: PM wants to explore motivations before designing a concept test User: /persona ask What frustrates you most about choosing skincare products? Agent: Each persona answers independently in its own subprocess, then themes are synthesized across responses with representative verbatims. </example>
<example> Context: Team needs to compare three product concepts side by side User: /persona concept-test Compare 3 skincare concepts for Gen Z. A: Acne Control Serum. B: Barrier Repair Cream. C: Glow Boosting Toner. Agent: Runs structured A/B/C concept test, returns first-choice counts, purchase likelihood means, segment × choice cross-tabs, and verbatims. </example>

- code-reviewer: Expert code review specialist. Proactively reviews code for quality, security, and maintainability. Use immediately after writing or modifying code.
- desktop-app-dev
- architect: Deep technical work. Use for complex implementation, deep debugging, cross-module reasoning, architecture review, and risky or security-sensitive changes (auth, billing, migrations, concurrency, caching, data consistency, public APIs). Also reviews work from cheaper agents for hidden flaws.
- executor: Standard engineering execution. Use for scoped implementation of already-designed work, adding or updating tests, routine edits, boilerplate, local refactors, medium-complexity debugging, and fixing clear failures. Does not make product calls or change architecture.
- scout: Cheap evidence gathering. Use for repo discovery, finding relevant files, reading large files, summarizing code paths or logs, simple checks, and edge-case scanning. Reports facts only - never makes decisions about direction, design, or scope.
- verifier: Independent evidence-based verification. Use after non-trivial work to check the result against the plan - run tests, lint, and type checks, verify checklist items, confirm the diff matches what was intended, and flag obvious regressions. Reports pass/fail with evidence; never fixes anything.
- code-architect: Designs feature architectures by analyzing existing codebase patterns and conventions, then providing comprehensive implementation blueprints with specific files to create/modify, component designs, data flows, and build sequences
- code-explorer: Deeply analyzes existing codebase features by tracing execution paths, mapping architecture layers, understanding patterns and abstractions, and documenting dependencies to inform new development
- forkmind-debugger: Drives ForkMind to debug, compare, or regression-test LLM / agent calls in an isolated context. Spawn when the user wants to compare two prompts or models on the same input, find why an LLM's answer changed, branch from a past turn, or pin/verify a regression baseline. Returns a compact verdict (winner, diffs, drift) — not raw transcripts — so main context stays small.

- x-twitter-scraper: Use this agent for X data research, lookups, extraction, monitoring, and exports. It also supports approval-gated actions through Xquik. Not affiliated with X Corp.
- a11y-architect: Accessibility Architect specializing in WCAG 2.2 compliance for Web and Native platforms. Use PROACTIVELY when designing UI components, establishing design systems, or auditing code for inclusive user experiences.
- agent-evaluator: Evaluates agent output against 5-axis quality rubric (accuracy, completeness, clarity, actionability, conciseness). Use after any non-trivial task when the user wants a quality assessment, or when the agent-self-evaluation skill is active. Produces structured scorecard with evidence and improvement suggestions.
- build-error-resolver: Build and TypeScript error resolution specialist. Use PROACTIVELY when build fails or type errors occur. Fixes build/type errors only with minimal diffs, no architectural edits. Focuses on getting the build green quickly.
- chief-of-staff: Personal communication chief of staff that triages email, Slack, LINE, and Messenger. Classifies messages into 4 tiers (skip/info_only/meeting_info/action_required), generates draft replies, and enforces post-send follow-through via hooks. Use when managing multi-channel communication workflows.
- code-simplifier: Simplifies and refines code for clarity, consistency, and maintainability while preserving behavior. Focus on recently modified code unless instructed otherwise.
- comment-analyzer: Analyze code comments for accuracy, completeness, maintainability, and comment rot risk.
- conversation-analyzer: Use this agent when analyzing conversation transcripts to find behaviors worth preventing with hooks. Triggered by /hookify without arguments.
- cpp-build-resolver: C++ build, CMake, and compilation error resolution specialist. Fixes build errors, linker issues, and template errors with minimal changes. Use when C++ builds fail.
- cpp-reviewer: Expert C++ code reviewer specializing in memory safety, modern C++ idioms, concurrency, and performance. Use for all C++ code changes. MUST BE USED for C++ projects.
- csharp-reviewer: Expert C# code reviewer specializing in .NET conventions, async patterns, security, nullable reference types, and performance. Use for all C# code changes. MUST BE USED for C# projects.
- dart-build-resolver: Dart/Flutter build, analysis, and dependency error resolution specialist. Fixes `dart analyze` errors, Flutter compilation failures, pub dependency conflicts, and build_runner issues with minimal, surgical changes. Use when Dart/Flutter builds fail.
- database-reviewer: PostgreSQL database specialist for query optimization, schema design, security, and performance. Use PROACTIVELY when writing SQL, creating migrations, designing schemas, or troubleshooting database performance. Incorporates Supabase best practices.
- django-build-resolver: Django/Python build, migration, and dependency error resolution specialist. Fixes pip/Poetry errors, migration conflicts, import errors, Django configuration issues, and collectstatic failures with minimal changes. Use when Django setup or startup fails.
- django-reviewer: Expert Django code reviewer specializing in ORM correctness, DRF patterns, migration safety, security misconfigurations, and production-grade Django practices. Use for all Django code changes. MUST BE USED for Django projects.
- doc-updater: Documentation and codemap specialist. Use PROACTIVELY for updating codemaps and documentation. Generates docs/CODEMAPS/*, updates READMEs and guides. Backs the /update-codemaps and /update-docs commands.
- docs-lookup: When the user asks how to use a library, framework, or API or needs up-to-date code examples, use Context7 MCP to fetch current documentation and return answers with examples. Invoke for docs/API/setup questions.
- e2e-runner: End-to-end testing specialist using Vercel Agent Browser (preferred) with Playwright fallback. Use PROACTIVELY for generating, maintaining, and running E2E tests. Manages test journeys, quarantines flaky tests, uploads artifacts (screenshots, videos, traces), and ensures critical user flows work.
- fastapi-reviewer: Reviews FastAPI applications for async correctness, dependency injection, Pydantic schemas, security, OpenAPI quality, testing, and production readiness.
- flutter-reviewer: Flutter and Dart code reviewer. Reviews Flutter code for widget best practices, state management patterns, Dart idioms, performance pitfalls, accessibility, and clean architecture violations. Library-agnostic — works with any state management solution and tooling.
- fsharp-reviewer: Expert F# code reviewer specializing in functional idioms, type safety, pattern matching, computation expressions, and performance. Use for all F# code changes. MUST BE USED for F# projects.
- gan-evaluator: GAN Harness — Evaluator agent. Tests the live running application via Playwright, scores against rubric, and provides actionable feedback to the Generator.
- gan-generator: GAN Harness — Generator agent. Implements features according to the spec, reads evaluator feedback, and iterates until quality threshold is met.
- gan-planner: GAN Harness — Planner agent. Expands a one-line prompt into a full product specification with features, sprints, evaluation criteria, and design direction.
- go-build-resolver: Go build, vet, and compilation error resolution specialist. Fixes build errors, go vet issues, and linter warnings with minimal changes. Use when Go builds fail.
- go-reviewer: Expert Go code reviewer specializing in idiomatic Go, concurrency patterns, error handling, and performance. Use for all Go code changes. MUST BE USED for Go projects.
- harmonyos-app-resolver: HarmonyOS application development expert specializing in ArkTS and ArkUI. Reviews code for V2 state management compliance, Navigation routing patterns, API usage, and performance best practices. Use for HarmonyOS/OpenHarmony projects.
- harness-optimizer: Improve local agent-harness configuration reliability and cost using eval-driven grading (pass@k/pass^k) derived from the eval-harness skill.
- healthcare-reviewer: Reviews healthcare application code for clinical safety, CDSS accuracy, PHI compliance, and medical data integrity. Specialized for EMR/EHR, clinical decision support, and health information systems.
- homelab-architect: Designs home and small-lab network plans from hardware inventory, goals, and operator experience level, with safe staged changes and rollback guidance.
- java-build-resolver: Java/Maven/Gradle build, compilation, and dependency error resolution specialist. Automatically detects Spring Boot or Quarkus and applies framework-specific fixes. Fixes build errors, Java compiler errors, and Maven/Gradle issues with minimal changes. Use when Java builds fail.
- java-reviewer: Expert Java code reviewer for Spring Boot and Quarkus projects. Automatically detects the framework and applies the appropriate review rules. Covers layered architecture, JPA/Panache, MongoDB, security, and concurrency. MUST BE USED for all Java code changes.
- kotlin-build-resolver: Kotlin/Gradle build, compilation, and dependency error resolution specialist. Fixes build errors, Kotlin compiler errors, and Gradle issues with minimal changes. Use when Kotlin builds fail.
- kotlin-reviewer: Kotlin and Android/KMP code reviewer. Reviews Kotlin code for idiomatic patterns, coroutine safety, Compose best practices, clean architecture violations, and common Android pitfalls.
- loop-operator: Operate autonomous agent loops, monitor progress, and intervene safely when loops stall.
- marketing-agent: Marketing strategist and copywriter for campaign planning, audience research, positioning, copy creation, and content review. Covers landing pages, email sequences, social posts, ad copy, short-form video scripts, and content calendars. Use when the user wants to plan or execute a product launch or marketing campaign.
- mle-reviewer: Production machine-learning engineering reviewer for data contracts, feature pipelines, training reproducibility, offline/online evaluation, model serving, monitoring, and rollback. Use when ML, MLOps, model training, inference, feature store, or evaluation code changes.
- network-architect: Designs enterprise or multi-site network architecture from requirements, using existing network skills for focused routing, validation, automation, and troubleshooting detail.
- network-config-reviewer: Reviews router and switch configurations for security, correctness, stale references, risky change-window commands, and missing operational guardrails.
- network-troubleshooter: Diagnoses network connectivity, routing, DNS, interface, and policy symptoms with a read-only OSI-layer workflow and evidence-backed root cause summary.
- opensource-forker: Fork any project for open-sourcing. Copies files, strips secrets and credentials (20+ patterns), replaces internal references with placeholders, generates .env.example, and cleans git history. First stage of the opensource-pipeline skill.
- opensource-packager: Generate complete open-source packaging for a sanitized project. Produces CLAUDE.md, setup.sh, README.md, LICENSE, CONTRIBUTING.md, and GitHub issue templates. Makes any repo immediately usable with Claude Code. Third stage of the opensource-pipeline skill.
- opensource-sanitizer: Verify an open-source fork is fully sanitized before release. Scans for leaked secrets, PII, internal references, and dangerous files using 20+ regex patterns. Generates a PASS/FAIL/PASS-WITH-WARNINGS report. Second stage of the opensource-pipeline skill. Use PROACTIVELY before any public release.
- performance-optimizer: Performance analysis and optimization specialist. Use PROACTIVELY for identifying bottlenecks, optimizing slow code, reducing bundle sizes, and improving runtime performance. Profiling, memory leaks, render optimization, and algorithmic improvements.
- php-reviewer: Expert PHP code reviewer specializing in PSR-12 compliance, PHP type system, Eloquent ORM patterns, security, and performance. Use for all PHP code changes. MUST BE USED for PHP projects.
- planner: Expert planning specialist for complex features and refactoring. Use PROACTIVELY when users request feature implementation, architectural changes, or complex refactoring. Automatically activated for planning tasks.
- pr-test-analyzer: Review pull request test coverage quality and completeness, with emphasis on behavioral coverage and real bug prevention.
- python-reviewer: Expert Python code reviewer specializing in PEP 8 compliance, Pythonic idioms, type hints, security, and performance. Use for all Python code changes. MUST BE USED for Python projects.
- pytorch-build-resolver: PyTorch runtime, CUDA, and training error resolution specialist. Fixes tensor shape mismatches, device errors, gradient issues, DataLoader problems, and mixed precision failures with minimal changes. Use when PyTorch training or inference crashes.
- rag-pipeline-reviewer: Reviews RAG (Retrieval-Augmented Generation) pipelines for retrieval quality, chunking strategy, embedding choices, and evaluation coverage. Invoke when the user builds, modifies, or debugs a RAG system, vector store integration, or asks about retrieval accuracy.
- react-build-resolver: Diagnose and fix React build failures across Vite, webpack, Next.js, CRA, Parcel, esbuild, and Bun. Handles JSX/TSX compile errors, hydration mismatches, server/client component boundary failures, missing types, and bundler-specific configuration issues with minimal, surgical changes. MUST BE USED when a React build fails.
- react-reviewer: Expert React/JSX code reviewer specializing in hook correctness, render performance, server/client component boundaries, accessibility, and React-specific security. Use for any change touching .tsx/.jsx files or React component logic. MUST BE USED for React projects.
- refactor-cleaner: Dead code cleanup and consolidation specialist. Use PROACTIVELY for removing unused code, duplicates, and refactoring. Runs analysis tools (knip, depcheck, ts-prune) to identify dead code and safely removes it.
- rust-build-resolver: Rust build, compilation, and dependency error resolution specialist. Fixes cargo build errors, borrow checker issues, and Cargo.toml problems with minimal changes. Use when Rust builds fail.
- rust-reviewer: Expert Rust code reviewer specializing in ownership, lifetimes, error handling, unsafe usage, and idiomatic patterns. Use for all Rust code changes. MUST BE USED for Rust projects.
- security-reviewer: Security vulnerability detection and remediation specialist. Use PROACTIVELY after writing code that handles user input, authentication, API endpoints, or sensitive data. Flags secrets, SSRF, injection, unsafe crypto, and OWASP Top 10 vulnerabilities.
- seo-specialist: SEO specialist for technical SEO audits, on-page optimization, structured data, Core Web Vitals, and content/keyword mapping. Use for site audits, meta tag reviews, schema markup, sitemap and robots issues, and SEO remediation plans.
- silent-failure-hunter: Review code for silent failures, swallowed errors, bad fallbacks, and missing error propagation.
- spec-miner: Extracts behavioral specs from existing codebases for OpenSpec. Produces flat Requirement and Invariant blocks with structured metadata (entities, enforced, id, test anchors). Outputs openspec/specs/<capability>/spec.md. Fully self-bootstrapping — no dependency on codebase-onboarding. Use when onboarding a brownfield project to spec-driven development.
- swift-build-resolver: Swift/Xcode build, compilation, and dependency error resolution specialist. Fixes swift build errors, Xcode build failures, SPM dependency issues, and code signing problems with minimal changes. Use when Swift builds fail.
- swift-reviewer: Expert Swift code reviewer specializing in protocol-oriented design, value semantics, ARC memory management, Swift Concurrency, and idiomatic patterns. Use for all Swift code changes. MUST BE USED for Swift projects.
- tdd-guide: Test-Driven Development specialist enforcing write-tests-first methodology. Use PROACTIVELY when writing new features, fixing bugs, or refactoring code. Ensures 80%+ test coverage.
- type-design-analyzer: Analyze type design for encapsulation, invariant expression, usefulness, and enforcement.
- typescript-reviewer: Expert TypeScript/JavaScript code reviewer specializing in type safety, async correctness, Node/web security, and idiomatic patterns. Use for all TypeScript and JavaScript code changes. MUST BE USED for TypeScript/JavaScript projects.
- vue-reviewer: Expert Vue.js code reviewer specializing in Composition API correctness, reactivity pitfalls, component architecture, template security, and Vue-specific performance. Use for any change touching .vue, .ts/.js files with Vue imports, or Vue ecosystem code (Pinia, Vue Router, Nuxt). MUST BE USED for Vue projects.
- API Design Expert: Senior API architect specializing in RESTful design, GraphQL schemas, and API-first development

<dynamic_tool_catalog>
These dynamic tool namespaces were available when this conversation started. Availability may have changed, so use `GetDynamicTools` to check current state before calling `CallDynamicTool`.

<dynamic_tool_namespaces>
<namespace name="cursor-app-control" tools="move_agent_to_root, move_agent_to_cloned_root, create_project, rename_chat, cursor_dialog, open_resource, open_automation, install_plugin" namespaceUseInstructions="Controls the Cursor application itself (workspace root, projects, opening resources, automations, and local task drafts, user rules, chat title). See each tool description for usage." source="mcp" />
<namespace name="cursor-ide-browser" tools="browser_navigate, browser_snapshot, browser_click, browser_mouse_click_xy, browser_type, browser_fill, browser_select_option, browser_press_key, browser_scroll, browser_drag, browser_get_bounding_box, browser_highlight, browser_tabs, browser_cdp, browser_take_screenshot, browser_lock" namespaceUseInstructions="The cursor-ide-browser MCP server provides a Cursor-owned browser tab plus a raw Chrome DevTools Protocol command tool.

CORE WORKFLOW:
1. Start by understanding the user's goal and what success looks like on the page.
2. Use browser_tabs with action "list" to inspect open tabs and URLs before acting.
3. Use browser_navigate to create or navigate the target tab. Omit the position parameter for background automation so focus is preserved.
4. Use browser_lock before longer automation on an existing tab, then browser_lock with action "unlock" when finished.
5. Use browser_snapshot for accessibility context and browser_take_screenshot for visual verification.
6. Use browser_click, browser_type, browser_fill, browser_select_option, browser_press_key, browser_scroll, and browser_drag for page interactions.
7. Use browser_highlight and browser_get_bounding_box for visual grounding and coordinate diagnostics.
8. Use browser_cdp for page inspection, profiling, runtime evaluation, DOM/CSS queries, and performance data.

AVOID RABBIT HOLES:
1. Do not repeat the same failing action more than once without new evidence such as a fresh snapshot, a different ref, a changed page state, or a clear new hypothesis.
2. IMPORTANT: If four attempts fail or progress stalls, stop acting and report what you observed, what blocked progress, and the most likely next step.
3. Prefer gathering evidence over brute force. If the page is confusing, use browser_snapshot, browser_take_screenshot, or CDP inspection before trying more actions.
4. If you encounter a blocker such as login, passkey/manual user interaction, permissions, captchas, destructive confirmations, missing data, or an unexpected state, stop and report it instead of improvising repeated actions.
5. Do not get stuck in wait-action-wait loops. Every retry should be justified by something newly observed.

CRITICAL - Lock/unlock workflow:
1. browser_lock requires an existing browser tab - you CANNOT call browser_lock with action: "lock" before browser_navigate
2. Correct order: browser_navigate -> browser_lock({ action: "lock" }) -> (interactions) -> browser_lock({ action: "unlock" })
3. If a browser tab already exists (check with browser_tabs list), call browser_lock with action: "lock" FIRST before any interactions
4. Only call browser_lock with action: "unlock" when completely done with ALL browser operations for this turn

IMPORTANT - Waiting strategy:
When waiting for page changes, prefer short CDP polling loops with Runtime.evaluate, DOM queries, Page lifecycle signals, or browser_snapshot checks rather than a single long wait.

CDP USAGE:
- Use browser_cdp with a DevTools Protocol method and params object, for example Runtime.evaluate, DOM.getDocument, CSS.getComputedStyleForNode, Profiler.start/stop, Performance.getMetrics, Log.enable, and Network.enable.
- Do not use browser_cdp with CDP Input.* methods. They are denied because they are focus-sensitive in Electron webviews and can route input to Cursor UI instead of the browser page.
- Use browser_click, browser_type, browser_fill, browser_select_option, browser_press_key, browser_scroll, and browser_drag for clicks, typing, filling inputs, selecting options, keyboard actions, scrolling, and drag-and-drop.
- Use Runtime.evaluate for advanced DOM-scoped interactions that the dedicated browser tools do not cover.
- For profiling, call Profiler.enable, Profiler.start, reproduce the behavior, then Profiler.stop. The profile is saved to a file and returned as a log_file; read that file only when you need to inspect details.
- For JavaScript evaluation, prefer Runtime.evaluate with returnByValue when possible.
- Some browser-wide or sensitive CDP methods are denied, especially cookie, storage, permission, download, target-management, filesystem-backed file-input commands, system-level commands, and CDP navigation/history navigation commands.
- Large CDP responses are saved to files instead of being inlined. Prefer using the returned file path over immediately stuffing large payloads into context; read focused sections only when needed.

VISION:
- browser_take_screenshot attaches an image result that the model can inspect. CDP Page.captureScreenshot returns data inside JSON and should not replace browser_take_screenshot when visual verification is needed.

NOTES:
- browser_snapshot returns snapshot YAML and is the main source of truth for page structure.
- Refs are opaque handles tied to the latest browser_snapshot for that tab.
- Iframe content is not accessible - only elements outside iframes can be interacted with.
- When you stop to report a blocker, include the current page, the target you were trying to reach, the blocker you observed, and the best next action. If the blocker requires manual user interaction, ask the user to take over at that point rather than assuming it in advance." source="mcp" />

### 🤖 Agente

My new border rules should override the existing gameboy theme border styles since they come later with equal specificity. But I realize the modal's overflow:hidden will clip the ::after pseudo-element positioned below the bottom edge, which is a problem for the bottom border detail.

I'll add an override so gameboy-themed modals use overflow:visible instead, since the grid rows already handle scrolling within modal-body. Let me add that rule and move on to running simulation tests.

For Perfil, the attributes grid is overflowing outside the scroll panel so only half the stats show, and the menu seems to dim unexpectedly — I need to check the Perfil code in ui.js to see why it's fading and clipping.

### 🤖 Agente

I'm noticing the profile dl already has its own scrollbar, suggesting the .facts element has a max-height causing overflow and clipping attributes. I should check the .facts CSS to see if wrapping everything in a single scroll container would be simpler.

### 🤖 Agente

The menu panel already scrolls, so the remaining attributes are just further down. Checking the other screenshots:

The Game Over and the roles dialog both work. The ball scene shows two balls (one in the pet's hands plus the physical one), so I'm checking that:

That's the pet picking the ball up to throw it, which is expected. Checking the hook and the leave scene:

That ball-in-hands pose is probably just the pet carrying the ball before throwing it, which seems like a legitimate action. I'll accept it and move on quickly.

The pet leaving works. The hook chain points down even though the target is up and to the right, so I'm looking at how the chain is drawn:

The chain is pointing down-right instead of up-right toward the target, so there might be a sign error in the rotation calculation or a coordinate mismatch between world and screen space for the pet's y position. I need to check the chain rendering logic in pet.js.

I'm not totally sure why that floor icon is there; it could be a stale overlay from the self-pull animation rather than an inventory item like a gear or toy. I should check the self-pull code around where the arrival and hand positioning logic is defined to understand what's being drawn.

I'm reconsidering the feet positioning — moving feet straight to the tip means the hand ends up above it with the chain pointing down, which looks off. Instead I should offset the target so the hookOrigin itself lands on the tip, accounting for the hand-to-origin distance, so feet settle correctly when the hook arrives at a surface like the window top.

Simpler approach: have the feet move toward a hang point below the hook, then snap directly to the tip once they're within the final step distance, effectively climbing up at the end.


I'll adjust the animation so the pet hangs below the hook during the pull and only snaps onto the tip at the very end, rather than having feet move to the tip early (which pushes the hand above the tip and flips the chain).

### 🤖 Agente

As 29 mudanças estão implementadas, o app compilou e a versão nova já está aberta (`dist\Desktop Pet\Desktop Pet.exe`). O simulador rodou com 0 erros, mas os testes no navegador headless deram só uma olhada rápida em cada recurso. Vale você testar no uso real, principalmente a importação de VRoid: nenhum modelo `.vrm` de verdade foi testado.

**O que conferi nas capturas:**
- **Game Boy:** o pet pega o console antes de jogar, segura de costas e a tela do jogo fica parada no lugar.
- **Bola:** o pet chuta e arremessa, e aparece a reação de empolgação.
- **Gancho:** a corrente e a ponta aparecem e o pet é puxado até o alvo. Corrigi um erro: no fim da puxada a corrente apontava para baixo. Agora o pet fica pendurado abaixo do gancho.
- **Afeição baixa:** o pet faz a trouxinha, vai embora e aparece a tela preta de GAME OVER só com "Tentar denovo . . .".
- **Menus:** Perfil com Afeição e atributos (os de baixo aparecem rolando o painel), barra de Sede, temas GB/GBC/GBA/GB Light com borda de console, o editor de animações com prévia ao lado e o filtro CRT.

Adicionei esses cenários de teste em `test/ui.html`.

**Sobre o `Vocaloid_Miku_1.ysm`:** não vou tentar quebrar a criptografia desse arquivo. É uma proteção (DRM) colocada pelo autor do modelo. As alternativas são:
- exportar o modelo pelo próprio mod YSM como pasta ou `.zip` e importar pela opção "Pasta do modelo";
- pedir ao autor a versão sem criptografia;
- usar um modelo VRoid (`.vrm`) da Miku, que o app agora importa com expressões.

It's already built and running, so I can leave the http server as is. Now I'll put together the final report concisely in pt-BR.

### 🤖 Agente

Está tudo implementado, e o app foi recompilado e reaberto (`dist\Desktop Pet\Desktop Pet.exe`). Ele iniciou sem erros no log. O simulador passou com 0 erros, e conferi nos testes do Edge headless os móveis, a TV com o jogo rodando na tela, a aproximação da tela, o status fixo e os diálogos de Objetos e de Resourcepack. Ainda não foram testados com arquivos reais: importação de resourcepack, GIF, modelo 3D e skin do Minecraft.

**Importação**
- **Importar modelo:** antes de escolher o arquivo você diz se ele vai para o **Pet** ou para o **Doll**. As opções são pasta YSM, .zip, **Modelo 3D (.vrm/.glb)** e **Skin do Minecraft (.png)**. A skin tem pré-visualização animada e detecta sozinha se é do tipo Alex (braços finos).
- **Bug do YSM corrigido:** clicar em outra textura não faz mais o modelo sumir.
- **Remover acessórios:** dá para desmarcar partes do modelo tanto na importação quanto depois, em Modelo → Acessórios.
- **Resourcepack (MC 1.20.1):** fica em Temas → Importar resourcepack. Você escolhe as partes (janelas, itens, sons, músicas, mobília) com uma prévia ao lado.
  - A mochila de comida vira barril e a bolsa de cartuchos vira baú.
  - Os temas escolhidos têm prioridade sobre as janelas do resourcepack.
  - As músicas tocam pelo menu do botão direito do kit disco.
- **Visual dos objetos:** cada objeto pode receber PNG, GIF (com velocidade) ou modelo 3D (com rotação, inclinação ou girando), com prévia. Isso tem prioridade sobre o resourcepack. Na TV, você marca a área da tela arrastando sobre a imagem.

**Jogo**
- **Móveis:**
  - Cama, onde o pet dorme mais e recupera energia em dobro.
  - Almofada.
  - VideoGame: TV + NES, com o jogo aparecendo na tela da TV.
  - Estante com 40 mangás.
  - Alvo: o pet joga dardos, e clicando você joga um minigame de 3 minutos com recorde.
  - Kit disco.
- **Consoles:** Game Gear e PSP 3000. O pet coloca o cartucho antes de jogar.
- **Bolas:** vôlei, basquete e queimada, cada uma com um jeito de jogar. A espada também rebate a bola.
- **Doll:** substitui a pelúcia. É um boneco articulado com física, e você pode arrastar e arremessar. A skin vem do Minecraft ou de um modelo 3D.
- **Pet vem até a tela:** ele cresce e às vezes bate no vidro. Pode ser desligado em Opções.
- **Status fixo na tela:** em Opções, escolha um dos quatro cantos.
- **Vários monitores:** os objetos ficam no monitor onde foram colocados. O pet não troca de monitor com o menu aberto ou enquanto brinca.
- **Botão direito em qualquer objeto:** abre opções como cor e intensidade do disco, skin do Doll, jogar no Alvo, mudar o visual e guardar no inventário.

Algumas coisas funcionam diferente do que você pode imaginar:
- Modelos 3D aplicados a objetos e ao Doll viram imagem (parada na rotação escolhida, ou girando), não 3D em tempo real.
- No resourcepack, só alguns itens têm equivalente no Minecraft. Os outros continuam com o visual padrão.

Tudo rodando sem erros no log. Vou deixar o servidor http ativo e relatar de forma concisa em pt-BR.
